Dear Vergel and Members of the Evaluation Committee,
Dear Members of the Evaluation Committee,
Propellant Media is pleased to submit our proposal in response to the Colorado School of Mines’ Request for Proposals (MINES26013) for Media Buying Management Services. We appreciate the opportunity to support Mines’ graduate enrollment goals through a strategic, data-driven paid media approach focused on increasing qualified leads, improving application outcomes, and maximizing return on investment.
We have also included a brief proposal video to provide additional context on our approach.
Colorado School of Mines is seeking more than a media buying vendor — you are seeking a transparent, performance-driven partner capable of managing and optimizing multi-channel campaigns across Google, LinkedIn, Meta, YouTube, and Reddit. We understand the complexity of managing multiple campaigns, supporting bundled program strategies, and aligning performance with long enrollment cycles and full-funnel outcomes.
Propellant Media specializes in lead generation and performance marketing, with a focus on advanced targeting, continuous optimization, and measurable impact. Our approach aligns directly with your priorities, including improving cost per lead and application, enhancing channel efficiency (particularly LinkedIn), and strengthening attribution to better connect media performance to enrollment outcomes.
Graduate enrollment conversion optimization differs significantly from traditional CRO due to longer decision cycles, multiple stakeholder influences, and the importance of micro-conversions (e.g., program engagement, content downloads) that precede inquiry and application. Our approach prioritizes optimizing these early and mid-funnel signals, not just final form submissions, to drive higher-quality inquiries and eventual enrollment outcomes.
What Differentiates Propellant Media for Colorado School of Mines:
Google Premier Partner — Top 3% of U.S. Agencies: Verified performance across client accounts, dedicated Google support, and early access to new campaign features — advantages that apply directly to Mines' Google Search and Performance Max campaigns.
Slate CRM Integration — Three-Step Framework: Pixel-to-Ping tracking on RFI forms, Audience Mirroring from Slate inquiry lists into Google and Meta, and Application Yield Reporting that syncs Slate application data with media spend to calculate true cost-per-admitted-student. Directly addresses Mines' stated attribution pain points from Q7 and Q50.
Application Push Strategy: A structured 8-week application conversion framework — coordinating paid retargeting, CRM urgency messaging, and bottom-funnel search campaigns timed to Mines' October–March deadlines. Most agencies stop at the RFI. We drive through to the application.
MWBE-Certified Partner: Federally certified Minority Business Enterprise through the NMSDC — supporting Mines' supplier diversity objectives.
Zero Upfront Costs: Full account transition, audit, pixel review, and dashboard setup are waived. Every dollar goes to working media from day one.
We approach this engagement as a strategic partnership, providing proactive recommendations, performance transparency, and ongoing optimization to support Mines’ enrollment growth objectives.
Thank you for your time and consideration. We look forward to the opportunity to partner with Colorado School of Mines.
Feel free to view the creative banner assets and video assets we developed below.
Thank you for your time and consideration.
Justin Croxton - CEO

MWBE-Certified & Mission-Aligned Partner
Propellant Media is a federally certified Minority Business Enterprise (MBE) via the National Minority Supplier Development Council (NMSDC). As Colorado School of Mines advances its mission of equitable access and inclusive community impact, our certification supports the University’s supplier diversity objectives while delivering proven performance.
Partnering with Propellant Media helps Colorado School of Mines:
Meet institutional and state-level MWBE procurement goals
Reinforce its public commitment to supplier inclusion and economic equity
Engage audiences with culturally resonant, multilingual, and equity-first messaging

<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="0" w:afterAutospacing="0" w:before="0" w:beforeAutospacing="0" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:ind w:left="720" w:hanging="360"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_8bpil1l48wzy" w:id="8"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="8"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Documentation and support for seamless transition at contract conclusion
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="0" w:beforeAutospacing="0" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:ind w:left="720" w:hanging="360"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_8bpil1l48wzy" w:id="8"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="8"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Flexibility to adapt strategies based on performance and institutional priorities
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_lhid9l9upefx" w:id="15"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="15"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Propellant Media confirms that all advertising accounts — Google, LinkedIn, Meta, Reddit, and all associated measurement accounts — are owned by Colorado School of Mines and remain the sole property of Mines during and after this engagement. Propellant Media operates as a user within Mines-owned accounts at all times and will never create or manage campaigns in agency-owned accounts.
Your Team
Propellant Media assigns a dedicated team structure to ensure consistent strategic oversight and operational execution throughout the engagement. Colorado School of Mines would be supported by a cross-functional team consisting of:
We have team members with direct collegiate expertise both working inside of universities in prior roles, as well as working with current universities at Propellant Media.  That will be reflected in our responses below along with the resumes of the team partnering with Colorado School of Mines.
Colorado School of Mines’s Dedicated Account Team
We understand the importance of assigning a dedicated and experienced team to manage Colorado School of Mines’s account effectively. Our approach emphasizes consistency, expertise, and a deep understanding of higher education marketing nuances. Below is our proposed team structure, assignment process, and strategies to ensure long-term success for Colorado School of Mines’s campaigns.
Team Composition and Roles
Project Manager (Team Member - Cris Burkhart)
Timeline Management: Utilizes Monday.com to monitor project progress, ensuring timely completion of tasks.
Workflow Coordination: Collaborates with Colorado School of Mines’s Integrated Marketing team to streamline processes.
Milestone Reporting: Provides regular updates on project milestones and promptly addresses any logistical challenges.
Currently overseeing accounts such as Kennesaw State University, Life University.
Our tool of choice for project management is Monday.com, which allows us to project manage all of our major enterprise accounts.  See sample screenshot below.
Media Planner/Account Manager (Alyssa Dixon, Brent LeVasseur)
Primary Liaison: Serves as the main point of contact between Colorado School of Mines and Propellant Media, ensuring clear and consistent communication.
Omnichannel Strategy Development: Crafts comprehensive media plans across platforms such as Google Ads, Meta (Facebook/Instagram), and programmatic.
Budget Allocation: Optimizes ad spend to maximize ROI while effectively reaching Colorado School of Mines’s target audiences.
Performance Optimization: Continuously analyzes campaign data to refine strategies and improve outcomes.
Strategic Oversight: Aligns campaign strategies with Colorado School of Mines’s objectives, overseeing execution and performance.
Quality Assurance: Ensures all deliverables meet the highest standards and align with Colorado School of Mines’s brand identity.
Currently working with Kennesaw State University, Stevens Tech, Florida Gulf Coast, and Arizona State University.
Google Ads and Meta Specialist (Juan Gomez, Justin Croxton)
Campaign Management: Oversees the creation, execution, and optimization of campaigns on Google Ads and Meta platforms.
Keyword and Audience Research: Conducts in-depth research to identify high-performing keywords and target audiences.
Ad Performance Analysis: Monitors and analyzes ad performance metrics, making data-driven adjustments to enhance effectiveness.
Currently working with Kennesaw State University and Northeast Ohio Medical University
Analytics, Data Integration & Reporting Specialist (Aaron King)
Data Mapping & Integration: Implements robust tracking systems to monitor campaign performance across all platforms.
Comprehensive Reporting: Prepares detailed reports on key performance indicators, providing insights into campaign effectiveness.
Strategic Recommendations: Offers data-driven suggestions for campaign optimization and future strategies.
CRM Integration: Pushing form submissions with digital attribution information into CRM so applicants and enrolled students can ultimately be tracked back to a marketing channel, source, campaign, keyword, etc.
Dashboard Integration: Syncing enriched form data with dashboard so we are not just reporting on pixel fires but also reporting on “source-of-truth” conversions.
Currently working with Kennesaw State University, Utah State University, and University of Arizona.
Assignment Process and Team Member Tenure (Justin Croxton, Brent LeVasseur)
Consistency and Continuity: We prioritize assigning team members to Colorado School of Mines’s account for a minimum of 1-2 years to build strong relationships and a deep understanding of Colorado School of Mines’s brand and objectives.
Onboarding and Training: Team members undergo comprehensive onboarding to familiarize themselves with Colorado School of Mines’s brand, goals, and the higher education landscape.
Performance Monitoring: Regular evaluations ensure team members meet performance standards and contribute effectively to the university’s success.
Creative & Brand Asset Develop (Diana Woodruff)
Oversees the full creative lifecycle—from initial concept through production and delivery—for static display, HTML5, and video ad assets tailored to Colorado School of Mines's target audiences.
Leads cross-functional collaboration between strategy, media, and design teams to align messaging with campaign objectives and ensure consistency with Colorado School of Mines's brand guidelines.
Implements A/B testing frameworks and data-informed creative iterations to enhance engagement, improve click-through rates, and drive RFIs and conversions across channels.
Currently working with Northeast Ohio Medical University
Programmatic Display Campaign Development & Optimizations (Dylan Meetze)
Leads the planning, building, and deployment of Colorado School of Mines's programmatic display campaigns, leveraging audience segments, geotargeting, and dynamic creatives.
Continuously monitors and optimizes campaigns in real time—adjusting bids, refining placements, and reallocating spend based on performance trends and lead quality metrics.Collaborates closely with analytics and creative teams to insights into actionable enhancements that increase CTR, lower CPL, and improve overall campaign efficiency.
Communication Process & Meeting Cadence
We believe frequent and structured communication is key to success. Our approach ensures Colorado School of Mines’s marketing managers receive timely insights, performance updates, and strategic recommendations.
Bi-Weekly Meetings – Regular check-ins with Colorado School of Mines’s marketing managers to review performance, upcoming initiatives, and campaign optimizations.
Real-Time Reporting Dashboard – Colorado School of Mines will have 24/7 access to a custom Looker Studio dashboard displaying lead generation, CPL, CPE, and conversion trends across programs.
Monthly Performance Reports & Strategy Reviews – Detailed breakdown of campaign results, optimizations, and budget allocation. Recommendations on shifting budgets between platforms based on enrollment goals.
Dedicated Support & Rapid Response – On-call support for urgent campaign adjustments. Slack/email communication for quick approvals and ad optimizations.

Propellant Media brings structure, accountability, and transparency to every dollar spent. For the Colorado School of Mines, we’ve built a media monitoring and budget oversight framework that ensures placements perform as promised—and that reporting is clear and actionable.
Digital Optimization & Real-Time Reporting
For Colorado School of Mines’s digital efforts, performance monitoring and media optimization happen daily.
24/7 dashboard access for real-time campaign tracking.
Daily checks on CTR, conversions, and engagement by channel.
Ongoing A/B testing for creative, messaging, and audience segments.
Mid-flight budget shifts to high-performing platforms within 48 hours.
Budget Oversight & Transparency
Our budgeting process is built for clarity and control:
Dedicated Colorado School of Mines Budget Manager: Oversees pacing, allocation, and ROI.
Monthly Budget Reconciliation: Planned vs. actual spend by channel and campaign.
Detailed Line-Item Reporting: Breaks down agency fees, media spend, vendor costs, and platform delivery.
Reserve Flexibility: 5–7% held in reserve for testing or reallocation.
Live Dashboards: Sync with Google Ads, Meta, Programmatic, and OTT platforms for instant visibility.
If the Colorado School of Mines requests direct platform reports, we can supply those at any time.
Insertion Orders & Media Buy Management
Propellant manages all insertion orders in-house using standardized templates tailored to Colorado School of Mines’s campaign objectives. We ensure:
All commitments align with strategy and calendar.
Inventory is confirmed and backed by vendor documentation.

This framework ensures the Colorado School of Mines receives performance accountability, budget control, and campaign clarity—every step of the way.
This detailed breakdown will allow the Colorado School of Mines to easily track all expenses back to specific campaigns and timeframes. Furthermore, all billing will align with the pricing structure outlined in the accepted proposal(s). To maintain transparency, all agency fees will be distinctly separated from ad spend in the reports, ensuring Colorado School of Mines has a clear and accurate understanding of all costs associated with its digital marketing efforts.
Media spend on platforms automatically feed into dashboards for quick and easier spot checking and budget reconciliation.  If a client asks for monthly spend directly from the platform, we can supply that, as well.   Below is a snapshot of our current budget reconciliation sheet we utilize with the university.
Project Budget Management Strategy
Propellant Media maintains rigorous internal processes to ensure every dollar spent generates measurable impact. For Colorado School of Mines, our budget management approach includes:
Dedicated Budget Manager: A senior member of our media team will oversee the full budget to ensure pacing, allocation, and performance goals are consistently tracked and optimized.
Monthly Budget Reconciliation: Each month, we reconcile planned versus actual spend across all channels and provide Colorado School of Mines with line-item visibility into media delivery, pacing, and lead performance.
Dynamic Reallocation Protocol: We apply a flexible reallocation system that allows us to shift funds—within 48 hours—to high-performing channels when others underdeliver.
With a 100% transparent means of sharing reporting in real time while integrating with CRM platforms like Slate, our analytics and reporting team at Propellant Media has the capacity to build out custom reporting and/or leverage one’s existing reporting infrastructure including GA4, Tapclicks, Google Looker Studio.  We can even integrate higher education various CRMs into our designated dashboards for tracking in one place including Workday, and others.
You are welcome to review this video that provides more context and live examples of the reporting and how we pull data from any CRM system into our dashboard for better visualization and attribution:
In this case for Colorado School of Mines, regardless of the CRM or analytics platform you utilize including Google Analytics or use our own internal dashboard platform.  All dashboards and reports will be tailored to reflect Colorado School of Mines’s enrollment priorities, including segmenting leads by program type and geographic region.Here are the benefits of our team and utilizing our real-time analytics infrastructure:
Multiple Colorado School of Mines team members can have access to reporting without having to log into each individual digital platform.
You will have 24/7 access to a dashboard at https://login.propellant.media
You can also receive bi-weekly reports sent straight to your inbox
We utilize Power BI, Google Looker Studio, Tapclicks and Tableau depending on the use case of the brand.
We can build out dashboards specific to each individual digital channel, including:
SEM: Google Ads, Microsoft Ads, YouTube, Google Display
Paid Social: Meta, LinkedIn, Snapchat
Programmatic Advertising: Audio, Site Retargeting, OTT/CTV, Hulu, Geofencing, Native Advertising, Pre-Rol Video
The various metrics we can and would report on includes:
Campaign Media Spends
Management fees
Impressions, frequency, clicks, ctr, cost per click, site visits
Walk-in Visits (Geofencing Campaigns)
Leads, Phone Calls and Conversions
Form Submissions
Application Submissions
Optimization insights (where available)
Overall Engagement analysis
Other industry-standard metrics
Here is an example we built for KSU’s Masters in Fintech program.  We have an executive summary, coupled with data from each digital channel of that program including:
Digital Channels: Google Ads, YouTube, Facebook/Instagram, Google Display Remarketing, Programmatic Display Remarketing, LinkedIn, Twitter (X), Call Tracking (using callrail)
Media Definitions (definitions what what certain metrics mean)
Media Targeting Parameters (audience targeting data, type of campaign targeting, etc… by channel)
Mines-Specific Reporting Framework
Every report Propellant Media delivers is built around Mines' stated success benchmarks, confirmed tech stack (GA4, GTM, Looker Studio, Tableau, Slate), and the full enrollment funnel from RFI to admitted students — not generic agency metrics.
Live Dashboard — 24/7 Access
Mines' Marketing Manager will have continuous access to a custom Looker Studio dashboard with four views:
Channel Performance: CTR, cost per RFI, and cost per application by platform — benchmarked against Mines' FY24-25 baselines (Google CTR: 6.54%, cost per application: $104.85; LinkedIn: $635.65)
Program Bundle View: RFI volume and cost per RFI by bundle — General Awareness, Mechanical Engineering, Electrical Engineering, ETM, Computer Science, Space Resources
Funnel Progression: RFIs → application starts → submitted applications, with Slate data synced to close the attribution gap confirmed in Addendum Q7 and Q50
Geographic Performance: Lead volume and cost efficiency by priority market — CO, CA, TX, East Coast
Monthly Report Structure
Delivered within 5 business days of month close. Every report follows the same structure so Mines' team always knows what to expect:
Executive summary: what happened, what it means, what changes next month
Performance vs. benchmark: every KPI against FY24-25 baseline and prior month
Optimization log: every budget shift, bid change, and campaign modification with documented rationale
A/B test results: what was tested, what the data showed, what is being implemented
Recommendations: 3–5 prioritized actions for the coming month tied to RFI volume and cost efficiency
Colorado School of Mines’s ability to convert prospective student interest into enrollment depends on more than impressions—it requires strong alignment between paid media campaigns, website engagement, and Mines' internal reporting systems. Propellant Media will operate as a strategic extension of Colorado School of Mines’s Marketing and Enrollment teams, ensuring that every qualified lead generated through paid social and digital campaigns is properly tracked, measured, and available for follow-up by Mines' admissions and enrollment teams.
We recognize that Mines' analytics and reporting infrastructure serves as the system of record for prospective student engagement. Our approach ensures that all advertising campaigns across Google, Meta, YouTube, and other digital platforms are structured with clear UTM tracking and conversion measurement. This framework allows Colorado School of Mines to see exactly how prospective students interact with campaign messaging—from first click through inquiry—providing transparent attribution and actionable marketing insight.
Segmented Audiences & Predictive Targeting
Our campaign strategy extends beyond broad awareness targeting. Using Colorado School of Mines’s existing campaign data, website analytics, and platform signals, we will develop audience segments designed to reach prospective students most likely to engage with the University’s programs. Examples include:
Lead engagement scoring based on ad clicks, video views, return visits, and inquiry form activity.
Program-level audience segmentation aligned with Colorado School of Mines’s academic and workforce development programs.
Lookalike audience models built from prior campaign engagement and website traffic, layered across Google, Meta, LinkedIn campaigns.
By combining digital engagement signals with geographic and demographic insights, Colorado School of Mines can prioritize outreach to audiences most likely to convert into inquiries or applications.
Attribution & Performance Visibility
Propellant Media will implement a clear attribution framework connecting paid media performance to measurable engagement outcomes. Our reporting structure provides the University with transparent insight into how campaigns contribute to prospective student engagement. This includes:
First-touch and multi-touch attribution within Google Analytics and advertising platforms.
Mapping UTM-coded campaign assets for accurate traffic and conversion tracking.
Stage-based reporting dashboards (impressions → website visit → inquiry)
Custom dashboards in Looker Studio to visualize performance by program, channel, and creative asset.
Performance monitoring to identify and address drop-off points in the inquiry process.
This approach moves beyond basic impression or click reporting—delivering the performance visibility Colorado School of Mines needs to optimize digital marketing and support enrollment growth.
Colorado School of Mines’s ability to translate prospective student interest into enrollment hinges on more than just impressions—it depends on seamless integration between digital campaigns and your CRM system. Propellant Media is prepared to serve as both a strategic and technical extension of Colorado School of Mines’s marketing and enrollment teams, ensuring every qualified lead generated through advertising is captured, enriched, and activated within Slate for timely follow-up and attribution.
We recognize that Slate or other CRMs are not simply a database — it is Mines' system of record for prospective student engagement. Our approach transforms Slate into an intelligence engine that reflects true student behaviors across Google, Meta, YouTube, streaming, and OOH campaigns. Every campaign asset we manage will carry UTM structures and identifiers tailored to the CRM’s taxonomy, ensuring transparent attribution from first click through enrollment.
Implementation Timeline (First-Year Focus)
Bottom line: We will ensure that every campaign dollar translates into actionable data inside Slate  or any CRM, giving Colorado School of Mines’s admissions and enrollment staff the intelligence they need to connect with prospective students at the right time, with the right message, across the right channels.
Sample Integration Experience - From Lead Forms To Any CRM Platform
Propellant Media utilizes an integrated workflow combining BigQuery, Google Sheets, and Zapier to automate and securely manage lead capture, enrichment, and delivery across platforms. This system allows us to collect, normalize, and route leads from multiple form sources—including Meta Lead Ads (Facebook/Instagram), LinkedIn Lead Gen, Formstack, SnapChat, and our proprietary Omnichannel Form—into both internal reporting systems and client CRM environments.
CRM Connection Overview Video -
https://www.loom.com/share/a73e2c053a864bb0bb4f6f1a5e8c3963?sid=50e69668-d630-4029-9BSU-0b8c4aaa489a
Each lead source is connected via Zapier, which triggers real-time or near real-time data pushes into a central Google Sheet configured as a data staging hub. From there, we apply formatting rules, field normalization, and deduplication to ensure that lead records meet the field structure required by Slate CRM (e.g., contact source, program interest, term, geolocation, etc.). The sheet is also configured to sync to BigQuery, where enriched lead data is cross-referenced with campaign metadata (channel, ad group, creative ID), allowing our team and the client to evaluate performance by source and audience segment.
Once verified, leads are sent securely to the client’s SFTP endpoint or CRM API for ingestion into CRM. We support daily and real-time delivery options and maintain field mapping documentation for transparency. In parallel, the data stored in BigQuery feeds our TapClicks dashboard, giving clients a live view of leads by form type, source, cost-per-lead, and conversion stage. This full-loop system ensures data integrity, campaign accountability, and seamless alignment between marketing and enrollment operations.
Closing the Loop: CRM Technical Integration
Colorado School of Mines is seeking a partner to help "strengthen measurement infrastructure". Propellant Media will not just track "clicks"; we will track "Enrolled Students" by integrating our media platforms directly with their CRM.
Our three-step Slate integration includes: 1. Pixel-to-Ping Tracking: Placing conversion pixels on Slate-hosted RFI forms to track inquiry sources in real-time. 2. Audience Mirroring: Uploading anonymized Slate inquiry lists into Google and Meta to create "Lookalike Audiences" of students most likely to apply. 3. Application Yield Reporting: Bi-weekly syncs between Slate application data and our media spend to calculate the true "Cost-per-Admitted-Student," not just "Cost-per-Click”.
We recognize that the effectiveness of Colorado School of Mines’ paid media campaigns extends beyond the click. Every prospective graduate student who engages with a campaign must land on a destination that efficiently converts interest into measurable actions such as Request for Information (RFI) submissions and application starts.
While Mines manages creative and landing page development internally, we understand that conversion performance is a critical driver of overall campaign success, particularly given the long enrollment cycle and emphasis on lead quality.
Alignment with Graduate Enrollment Strategy
Mines’ campaigns are structured around graduate program “bundles” and awareness initiatives, targeting prospective students across key markets and disciplines. Each audience segment—whether early-stage researchers or high-intent applicants—requires a tailored post-click experience that aligns with campaign messaging and enrollment goals.
Our role is to collaborate with the Office of Communications and Marketing to ensure that landing pages:
Maintain strong message alignment with ad campaigns
Support clear pathways to RFIs and application starts
Reflect program priorities and audience intent
Reinforce Mines’ brand and value proposition
Conversion Optimization Approach
We provide data-driven recommendations to improve landing page performance and conversion efficiency based on campaign insights.
Key areas of focus include:
Identifying friction points impacting conversion rates and lead quality
Recommending improvements to calls-to-action, form structure, and content hierarchy
Aligning landing page messaging with audience targeting and campaign intent
Supporting A/B testing strategies for headlines, layouts, and user experience
These recommendations are informed by ongoing campaign performance and user behavior data.
Testing & Continuous Improvement
Our optimization methodology includes structured testing and feedback loops between media performance and landing page experience.
We will:
Analyze conversion performance by channel, audience, and campaign
Recommend test priorities based on performance gaps and opportunities
Provide insights to inform internal development of landing page variations
Continuously refine recommendations based on measurable outcomes
This ensures landing page improvements are directly tied to campaign performance and ROI.
Attribution & Measurement Alignment
We align landing page performance with Mines’ existing analytics and CRM ecosystem, including GA4, Google Tag Manager, and Slate.
Our support includes:
Ensuring proper tracking of conversion events (RFIs, application starts)
Validating alignment between ad platforms and on-site tracking
Supporting improved visibility into lead quality and downstream outcomes
Providing insights to strengthen attribution across the full enrollment funnel
Strategic Value to Colorado School of Mines
While landing page execution remains internal, conversion optimization is a key lever for improving campaign efficiency. By aligning media strategy with landing page performance insights, Propellant Media ensures that advertising investments not only drive traffic, but also increase qualified inquiries, improve application rates, and enhance overall ROI.
Our role is to provide the data, insights, and strategic guidance needed to continuously improve conversion performance—supporting Mines’ broader goal of driving measurable graduate enrollment growth.
top platforms utilized for both undergraduate and graduate colleges.Kennesaw State
In a digitally competitive graduate enrollment market, Colorado School of Mines competes for a highly specific, technically-minded prospective student pool — one that is actively researching programs across multiple institutions simultaneously. Winning in this environment requires disciplined paid media governance, precision audience targeting, and a deep understanding of the STEM graduate enrollment funnel.
Mines Competes Nationally for Graduate Students in STEM and Engineering
Mines' primary competitive pressure comes from:
Public flagship universities with established STEM graduate reputations
Nationally ranked engineering and applied science programs
Large research universities with significantly larger advertising budgets
Online and hybrid master's programs competing on flexibility, speed, and price
Peer institutions across Colorado targeting the same Front Range prospect base
The Addendum confirms Mines' current priority markets as Colorado/Front Range, California, Texas, and the East Coast — meaning competition is not regional. It is national.
Representative Competitive Set — Graduate STEM and Engineering Programs

<w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar><w:vAlign w:val="top"/></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="000001D9"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">High CPCs in CS and AI keyword categories
Tuition figures represent approximate annual graduate program costs for AY 2024–2025. All institutions confirmed as primary graduate enrollment competitors per Mines Addendum Q101. CMU graduate tuition is shown as flat rate (~$28,710/semester per published schedule).
A note on tuition positioning: Mines sits at a notable disadvantage on sticker price versus several in-state and regional competitors. Out-of-state graduate tuition at Mines runs approximately $45,824 per year, placing it in the top 5% nationally for out-of-state graduate rates. Research.com Compared to Texas A&M ($24,505 out-of-state) and UT Austin ($22,954 out-of-state) — two of Mines' confirmed priority markets — this is nearly a 2x price differential. Paid media messaging must be sensitive to this gap and lean into Mines' institutional prestige, uniqueness, and career outcomes to justify the investment. This has direct implications for creative strategy, landing page recommendations, and audience qualification — all areas where Propellant Media will provide active guidance.
Key Competitive Pressures
1. Paid Search Saturation in STEM Keywords Competitors like CU Boulder, Purdue, and Northeastern aggressively bid on high-intent graduate terms such as:
"master's in mechanical engineering"
"online MS computer science"
"graduate engineering programs Colorado"
"master's in space resources" / "critical minerals programs"
This drives up cost-per-click in Mines' core program categories and demands a disciplined bidding strategy with layered audience signals.
2. LinkedIn Competition for Working Professionals LinkedIn is Mines' second-largest channel at 30% of spend. Peer institutions with larger budgets — particularly Northeastern, Purdue, and CMU — run robust LinkedIn campaigns targeting the same working professional and career-changer audiences Mines is pursuing in master's non-thesis programs.
3. Bundle vs. Program-Level Visibility Mines has strategically shifted from individual program promotion to awareness-based bundle campaigns (5–8 bundles across modalities). Competitors with larger budgets can still afford program-level targeting. This requires Mines' paid media partner to extract maximum efficiency from a bundled campaign architecture.
4. In-State Brand Equity Gap CU Boulder graduate tuition runs approximately $16,634 in-state and $38,802 out-of-state CollegeTuitionCompare — both lower than Mines — while carrying strong Front Range brand recognition. Colorado State's 2024–2025 graduate tuition is approximately $14,125 in-state and $30,813 out-of-state. CollegeTuitionCompare Both institutions represent direct price and brand competition in Mines' home market. Paid media must compensate by driving awareness that Mines is a graduate education provider, not just an undergraduate engineering school — a stated strategic priority in the Addendum.
5. Long Enrollment Cycle Complexity The graduate enrollment cycle for Mines averages 18 months. Competitors with sophisticated marketing automation and CRM attribution are nurturing prospects across this entire window. Mines' tracking infrastructure is described as "foundational" — creating an opportunity for a paid media partner who can help close the attribution gap between ad activity and Slate outcomes.
Strategic Opportunity for Colorado School of Mines
Mines holds institutional strengths that, when paired with precision paid media, create real competitive separation:
Unique positioning as the only major engineering-focused public research university in Colorado with a "big four" reputation at a smaller, more accessible scale (per Addendum Q14)
Strong existing Google Search performance — 6.54% CTR and 2,385 graduate applications at $104.85 per submitted application in FY24-25
Reddit video views overperforming — an underutilized channel where competitors are not yet saturated
Open to programmatic, CTV, and DOOH expansion — channels where Propellant Media brings direct capability
High-priority program alignment in energy, critical minerals, and quantum — areas with strong national and employer-driven demand
Through advanced audience segmentation, enrollment-cycle-aligned flighting, platform-level budget reallocation based on performance data, and a clear attribution bridge to Slate, Mines can compete effectively against institutions spending multiples of its current budget — and win on precision where it cannot win on volume.
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_7k1nt8i1h6l4" w:id="28"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="28"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Graduate enrollment at Colorado School of Mines does not operate on a simple awareness-to-application timeline. The Addendum confirms an 18-month average enrollment decision cycle — meaning the student who submits an RFI today may not apply for another year. This demands a paid media strategy built for sustained presence, not short-term bursts, with every channel selected to capture high-intent prospects when they are ready to act and maintain Mines' visibility among those still deciding.
The below graphic forms the framework in how Propellant Media executes media planning.
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_7k1nt8i1h6l4" w:id="28"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="28"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The channel mix is grounded in Mines' own performance data. Google Search delivers a 6.54% CTR and $104.85 cost per submitted application. LinkedIn generated 532 graduate applications at $635.65 per application — a higher but justified cost reflecting the quality of the working professional audience it reaches. The Reddit video is overperforming. Meta is gaining traction. This plan preserves those strengths while adding programmatic display and OTT/CTV to fill the gaps the current mix cannot address: sustained retargeting across the long decision cycle, brand lift measurement, and prospecting audiences not yet in Mines' pipeline.
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_j5g5k5enbwul" w:id="29"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="29"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The framework uses $650,000 as the planning figure — the midpoint of the stated $500,000–$800,000 range — and scales proportionally to the final approved budget. Graduate lead generation is the primary objective, with institutional brand awareness at approximately 10% of overall intent. Management fees are drawn from within the media budget, not on top of it. Budget flexibility across platforms is preserved, with Propellant Media bringing monthly reallocation recommendations as performance data dictates.

Mines already delivers a 6.54% CTR and $104.85 cost per submitted application through Google. Campaigns are organized around Mines' existing bundle architecture — General Graduate Awareness plus 5–8 program bundles — with separate ad groups per bundle targeting high-intent queries including "master's in mechanical engineering Colorado," "MS computer science programs," "master's in space resources," and "critical minerals graduate degree."
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_qda9lacjvd8s" w:id="30"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="30"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Google Search — Branded Defense (10% | $65,000) CU Boulder, CSU, Northeastern, and Purdue all have the budget to bid on Mines' branded terms. This allocation ensures Mines captures every high-intent prospect already searching by name. Without it, competitors intercept traffic generated by Mines' own awareness spend.

<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_qda9lacjvd8s" w:id="30"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="30"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">LinkedIn — Professional and Program Targeting (25% | $162,500) The confirmed vehicle for reaching working professionals pursuing master's non-thesis programs — Mines' primary growth target. Targeting leverages job function and seniority, industry (energy, defense, tech, aerospace, mining), employer-based targeting in CO, CA, TX, and East Coast markets, and retargeting against RFI non-converters. Separate campaigns built for each high-priority bundle: Mechanical Engineering, Electrical Engineering, Engineering & Technology Management, Computer Science, and Space Resources.

Combined with lookalike audiences built from Mines' Slate inquiry lists, this improves lead quality while containing cost per application.
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_s5jlyebe9w9j" w:id="31"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="31"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Programmatic — Dynamic Budget Allocation
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_s5jlyebe9w9j" w:id="31"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="31"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Our DSP uses machine learning to automatically shift impression share toward placements generating RFI activity and away from those that are not — within parameters reviewed weekly. This is critical for sustaining efficiency across an 18-month enrollment cycle where audience intent shifts seasonally.
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_tho76szapqa" w:id="32"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="32"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:b w:val="1"/><w:bCs w:val="1"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">The result: AI handles real-time bid and placement decisions at scale. Propellant Media's team focuses on the strategic layer — what to test, what the data means, and what recommendations to bring to Mines' team each month.
<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:bookmarkStart w:colFirst="0" w:colLast="0" w:name="_kiqoas8pnm23" w:id="33"/><w:bookmarkEnd w:id="33"/><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Paid media is not the sole enrollment driver — but a well-governed $650K investment at this efficiency level represents a meaningful contribution to Mines' targets of 5,313 applications and 1,700 enrolled graduate students, particularly for master's non-thesis programs where the most room to grow exists.
Creative Support & Optimization (Optional Add-On)
Colorado School of Mines has established strong in-house creative capabilities and will lead the development of the core campaign assets.  Propellant Media recognizes and respects this structure. Our proposed role, should the Colorado School of Mines wish to leverage additional support, would be to supplement, adapt, and optimize existing creative across digital, broadcast, print, and out-of-home channels to ensure that every placement achieves maximum performance. In this capacity, we would operate as a seamless extension of Colorado School of Mines’s Marketing team, working fully within established brand standards, accessibility guidelines, and the enterprise-wide identity system.
Sample Static Creative For Colorado School of Mines
View Creative Link Here
Video View Asset Link Here
Colorado School of Mines’s brand is already strong and widely recognized across the region. Propellant Media’s role is not to reinvent Colorado School of Mines’ creative, but to extend and amplify its voice across digital platforms. Our creative support focuses on adapting existing brand assets into channel-ready executions — display banners, social cutdowns, video edits, and retargeting formats — ensuring every impression reinforces Colorado School of Mines’s identity while maximizing media efficiency.

<w:left w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:bottom w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:right w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/><w:between w:color="auto" w:space="0" w:sz="0" w:val="none"/></w:pBdr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:color w:val="1f1f1f"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Mines has made clear that continuity is a priority. Campaigns are active, the enrollment cycle does not pause for a vendor transition, and the internal MarCom team is lean. Propellant Media's approach is built around that reality — a structured, phased transition that protects existing campaign performance on day one while systematically building toward the optimization and strategic depth that Mines currently lacks the bandwidth to execute. There is no ramp-up grace period that costs Mines leads. Work begins immediately.

A. Strategic Management & Media Buying Fees
Pricing & Investment Structure Colorado School of Mines — Media Buying and Management Services RFP MINES26013
Mines has indicated a clear preference for a commission-based, percentage-of-media-spend fee structure with no additional fees — confirmed in Addendum Q15 and Q94. Propellant Media's model is built precisely around that: a single management fee as a percentage of media spend, applied to a pass-through billing structure where Propellant Media pays ad platforms directly and issues Mines one itemized monthly invoice covering both media spend and management fee. No hidden costs. No platform markups. Every dollar accounted for and reconcilable against platform-level reporting.
Annual Investment Model
The RFP identifies an annual media budget of $509,000–$809,000 for July 2026–June 2027, with management fees drawn from within that budget. Three planning scenarios are provided across the full range.

Management fee is drawn from within the stated budget per Addendum Q27. Working media dollars represent the balance after fee extraction.
Pass-Through Billing
Propellant Media pays Google, LinkedIn, Meta, and Reddit directly on Mines' behalf. A single monthly invoice itemizes media spend by platform and management fee separately — consistent with Q111. All invoicing operates on NET 30 terms per Q35. Mines retains full ownership of all ad accounts at all times.
What the Management Fee Covers
We typically charge a one-time onboarding fee, however we will be waiving it in this instance.  Other services that is covered by our management fee includes:
Google Search, Performance Max, branded defense, bid optimization, search term mining
LinkedIn, Meta, and Reddit campaign management, audience targeting, A/B testing, and retargeting
Programmatic display, site retargeting, and OTT/CTV activation
24/7 live dashboard access, weekly optimization documentation, monthly strategy meetings, mid-cycle review, and full annual performance report
NOTE: Technology fee includes technology integration, Slate integrations to dashboard, UTM Tracking set up, and bi-directional data passthrough.
Account Transition & Setup — Waived
Propellant Media is waiving all account transition and setup fees for Colorado School of Mines. The full onboarding scope — campaign audit, conversion tracking validation, pixel review, Slate attribution assessment, and reporting dashboard configuration — is included at no additional cost.
This means 100% of Mines' budget goes directly to working media and management. There are no upfront costs, no setup charges, and no fees outside the 12% management rate. Mines produces all creative assets in-house, so no creative fees apply.

The 12% fee is fixed for the initial term. Any adjustment in subsequent years requires mutual agreement and would reflect scope changes — not standard escalators.
Pricing Rationale
Aligned to Mines' stated preference — percentage of spend, no additional fees, single itemized invoice per Q15, Q79, and Q94
Transparent and reconcilable — fee and media spend are distinct line items on every invoice and visible in real time on the dashboard
Media-weighted — 89 cents of every dollar at the $500K level goes directly to working media
No markups — platform costs are passed through at actual cost; management fee and media spend are always separate
No surprises — the 12% fee covers all services in scope and does not increase based on performance metrics or campaign volume
No upfront costs — account transition, audit, and setup are fully waived, meaning Mines' full budget is working from day one
Pricing Sheet Addendum
Below we list out six (6) case studies of undergraduate and graduate institutions or departments within those institutions that are relevant to understanding branding and enrollment management and digital marketing in higher education.  We have an additional case study that is west coast and government specific.
Kennesaw State University — MS in Electrical and Computer Engineering
Kennesaw State University's MS in Electrical and Computer Engineering program needed to grow qualified graduate inquiries from working professionals and recent engineering graduates — a highly specific, technically-minded audience with low tolerance for generic advertising. With a modest monthly media budget of approximately $1,600, the program could not afford broad awareness plays or wasted impressions. Every dollar needed to work.
The challenge was precision: reach the right engineers, in the right career stage, with messaging that spoke to the program's applied focus — without the budget scale that larger competitors deploy on the same keywords and the same LinkedIn audiences.
What Propellant Media Was Tasked With
Propellant Media was engaged to design and manage a paid media strategy that maximized lead volume and quality within the $1,600 monthly budget constraint. The mandate was clear: extract maximum efficiency from a lean investment by concentrating spend on the highest-intent channels and eliminating waste everywhere else.
Our Planning Approach
We deployed a two-channel strategy built around Google Search and LinkedIn — the channels most likely to reach engineers actively researching graduate programs or career advancement opportunities.
On Google, we built tightly structured search campaigns targeting high-intent queries including:
"master's in electrical engineering online"
"MS computer engineering program"
"graduate degree electrical engineering working professionals"
"KSU ECE graduate program"
Campaigns were structured with exact and phrase match control, continuous negative keyword refinement, and Smart Bidding optimized to RFI form submissions — not clicks. Every budget dollar was focused on converting search intent directly into inquiries.
On LinkedIn, we targeted by job function (engineering, information technology), seniority (associate through senior level), and field of study — concentrating spend on professionals in Georgia and surrounding markets whose background aligned directly with the program's applicant profile. Retargeting was layered on top to re-engage site visitors who had shown program interest but had not yet submitted an inquiry.
Budget pacing was managed weekly, with reallocation between channels based on which was generating the lower cost per qualified lead in any given period.
Campaign Results
$30 cost per lead — significantly below the graduate engineering program benchmark of $80–$120 CPL on Google and $140–$200 on LinkedIn
Consistent qualified inquiry volume sustained month over month within the $1,600 budget
Google Search and LinkedIn working in tandem — search capturing active program researchers, LinkedIn surfacing the program to career-ready professionals not yet in the consideration set
Campaign efficiency improved over time as Smart Bidding accumulated conversion data and negative keyword lists tightened
Why This Is Relevant to Mines
Mines' current Google cost per submitted application is $104.85 and LinkedIn cost per application is $635.65. The KSU ECE program demonstrates that with disciplined keyword architecture, Smart Bidding trained to conversion events, and precise LinkedIn audience segmentation, Propellant Media can drive graduate engineering inquiry costs well below standard benchmarks — even at modest budget levels. The same targeting discipline and budget governance we apply at $1,600 per month scales directly to Mines' $500,000–$800,000 annual investment, with compounding efficiency gains as conversion data accumulates across a larger campaign architecture.
A-B Tech Community College (NC Community Colleges)
Through a competitive RFP process, Propellant Media was selected by A-B Tech Community College in 2025 to support the launch and growth of the Behavioral Health Workforce Collaborative — a statewide initiative uniting A-B Tech, Forsyth Tech, and Stanly Community College to strengthen North Carolina’s behavioral health workforce pipeline.
While the initiative required brand development support, the core objective was clear:
Generate qualified student leads quickly through high-performance digital acquisition campaigns.
Strategic Objective
Launch a new workforce initiative across North Carolina
Drive immediate inquiry and application interest
Capture high-intent career-training searches
Build a scalable, performance-focused campaign model
The primary KPI: Qualified leads and program inquiries
Google Ads & Performance Max Strategy
Propellant Media deployed a search-first acquisition model supported by omnichannel reinforcement.
1. Google Search (High-Intent Capture)
We built tightly structured Google Search campaigns targeting:
“behavioral health training NC”
“substance abuse counselor certification”
“mental health technician program”
“behavioral health workforce training near me”
“community college counseling programs NC”
Campaign architecture included:
Exact and phrase match keyword segmentation
Geographic targeting across North Carolina
Conversion-optimized ad copy
Structured extensions for program credibility
Ongoing query mining and negative keyword refinement
Search served as the primary demand-capture engine.
2. Performance Max (Scale & Incremental Reach)
To expand beyond keyword-only targeting, we launched structured Performance Max campaigns designed to:
Capture incremental behavioral health career interest
Expand reach into adjacent healthcare audiences
Leverage Google’s intent signals for workforce-related queries
Asset groups were segmented by:
Career advancement messaging
Entry-level workforce training
Adult career-change audiences
PMAX accelerated volume while maintaining controlled CPL targets.
3. Meta & Site Retargeting (Conversion Reinforcement)
To improve conversion efficiency and recover non-converting traffic:
Meta campaigns targeted adults interested in healthcare careers
Dynamic site retargeting re-engaged users who visited program pages but did not convert
Creative variations reinforced career outcomes and workforce demand
This omnichannel layer strengthened the overall blended CPL and improved conversion rates.
Results (First 90 Days)
Within the first 90 days of campaign launch:
300+ qualified leads generated from Google Ads alone
Strong inquiry volume across all three partner institutions
Established a repeatable acquisition model for workforce program growth
The omnichannel campaign (Google Search + PMAX + Meta + Site Retargeting):
Increased total lead volume beyond search alone
Improved overall cost efficiency through retargeting reinforcement
Built sustained visibility for a newly launched initiative
Ongoing Impact
Today, Propellant Media continues to support A-B Tech and the Collaborative with:
Ongoing search optimization
Performance Max scaling
Audience refinement
Creative testing
Budget reallocation based on CPL performance
The result is a scalable, performance-driven enrollment engine powered primarily by Google Ads and Performance Max — reinforced through structured omnichannel support.
Today, Propellant Media continues to support AB Tech and the Behavioral Health Workforce Collaborative through ongoing creative services and digital strategy consulting.

KSU - Masters in Financial Technology (Fintech) Program
As a strategic partner with Propellant Media for 7 years, Kennesaw State University (KSU), Georgia’s third-largest university with over 43,000 students, partnered with Propellant Media to support enrollment growth for its graduate-level Financial Technology (Fintech) program. With increasing competition from regional and online-first institutions, the program needed to reach a highly specific audience while maintaining lead quality and cost efficiency.
Objectives
Increase qualified lead volume for the Fintech program across digital channels.
Implement lead capture mechanisms that prioritized intent and applicant readiness.
Improve tracking and attribution across upper and lower funnel tactics.
Optimize lead nurturing through a seamless post-conversion experience.
Strategic Approach
1. Full-Funnel, Omnichannel Execution
Propellant Media deployed a multi-platform campaign to attract, engage, and convert prospective students using high-precision targeting strategies:
Google Ads & Performance Max: Captured demand from users actively searching for fintech-related graduate programs.
Google Display & YouTube Remarketing: Maintained visibility and re-engaged site visitors through mid-funnel tactics.
Meta (Facebook & Instagram):
Focused on both acquisition and retargeting.
Leveraged interest- and behavior-based targeting to reach fintech-aligned prospects.
Integrated enhanced Instant Forms with features to screen for intent (see below).
LinkedIn Sponsored Messaging & Lead Gen Forms:
Used industry/job function targeting to reach working professionals in finance, tech, and related sectors.
Implemented custom creative and placements to drive direct lead capture.
Microsoft Ads: Supplemented search-based lead acquisition, particularly targeting older professionals.
OTT/CTV Campaigns: Reinforced awareness and brand recall at the top of the funnel through premium video placements.
2. Meta Lead Quality Optimization
To reduce form friction while increasing applicant quality, we implemented customizations to Meta Instant Forms, including:
A slider button that added a second layer of confirmation before form submission.
Custom question fields designed to require active user engagement.
A post-submit thank you page that drove immediate interaction with informational assets and next steps—enhancing lead nurturing and reducing drop-off.
This approach was intended to improve downstream engagement and improved MQL-to-application conversion rates.
3. CRM Integration & Attribution Tracking
Integrated all digital platforms with KSU’s CRM to ensure real-time lead delivery.
Built dashboards to track performance across search, social, OTT, and programmatic in one unified view.
Enabled attribution tracking on Meta and LinkedIn via conversion APIs and platform pixels.
Results (12-Month Period)
Total Leads Generated: 4,100+
Overall Cost Per Lead (CPL): $54
