Google Ads & PMAX generated 672 leads at a cost per lead of $38
LinkedIn campaigns proved particularly valuable for targeting high-intent professionals, despite higher CPL, and contributed meaningfully to lead quality.
Meta CPL outperformed benchmarks for graduate programs with significantly improved conversion behavior after implementing enhanced forms.
222
By executing a full-funnel campaign and innovating within lead capture platforms, Propellant Media helped KSU's Fintech program generate over 4,100 prospective students in 12 months while maintaining a strong focus on cost efficiency and applicant readiness. The custom lead form enhancements and strategic use of LinkedIn expanded both the volume and quality of inquiries—equipping the Coles College of Business with a reliable, scalable model for future graduate program recruitment.
Optimization in Action — KSU Fintech Program
In the second month of managing KSU's Fintech program, performance data showed LinkedIn generating leads at $70 CPL with a significantly higher application start rate than Meta leads at $38 CPL. Despite Meta's lower cost per lead, Slate CRM data revealed that Meta leads were converting to application starts at less than half the rate of LinkedIn leads — making the true cost per application start nearly equivalent across channels.
Based on this insight, we reallocated 20% of Meta budget to LinkedIn mid-flight. Over the following 60 days, application starts increased by 34% while blended CPL increased only marginally. By the end of the 12-month campaign, LinkedIn contributed 750 leads at $70 CPL — and those leads drove a disproportionate share of the program's total applications. The program went on to generate 4,100+ total leads and Propellant Media accounted for 75% of all applications generated for the Fintech program that year.
This is exactly the kind of data-driven reallocation we will apply to Mines — particularly the LinkedIn-to-application efficiency question that the Addendum explicitly identified as an area for improvement.
Worth noting that in 2025, our campaign accounted for 75% of all the applications generated for the Masters In Fintech Program.  See image above.
Utah State University (Online) Graduate Studies
Utah State University (USU) Online, a flagship public research institution serving more than 27,000 students, partnered with Propellant Media to increase enrollment across its fully online undergraduate and graduate degree programs.
USU Online required a digital marketing partner capable of elevating brand visibility for its online division while capturing high-intent prospective students searching for flexible, accredited online degree programs. The objective was not program-specific promotion alone, but scalable growth across USU’s full online portfolio.
Campaign Objectives
Increase qualified inquiries across all USU Online undergraduate and graduate programs
Strengthen brand visibility for USU Online as a flexible, accredited option
Capture high-intent “online degree” and “online university” search traffic
Improve full-funnel attribution during Salesforce CRM transition
Establish a repeatable Google Ads growth framework
Primary KPI: Qualified Leads (Inquiry Form Submissions)Secondary KPIs: Cost-Per-Lead (CPL), Conversion Rate, Application Progression
Google Ads Strategy
1. Brand + Non-Branded Search Architecture
Propellant Media developed a structured Google Search framework capturing both:
Branded Demand
Utah State Online
USU Online degrees
Utah State University online programs
USU online tuition
Non-Branded Online Degree Searches
accredited online degrees
online bachelor’s degree programs
online master’s programs
affordable online university
online college programs western U.S.
Campaigns were segmented by degree level (undergraduate vs. graduate) and optimized using:
Exact & phrase match keyword control
Geographic targeting aligned to recruitment priorities
Structured ad extensions driving to program category pages
Continuous search query mining
Negative keyword refinement
This ensured USU captured both existing brand interest and competitive online search traffic.
2. Performance Max for Portfolio-Wide Expansion
To extend reach beyond keyword-only targeting, we deployed Performance Max campaigns structured around:
Online Undergraduate Degrees
Online Graduate Programs
Flexible & Adult Learner Messaging
Performance Max leveraged Google’s automation and intent signals to identify users demonstrating interest in online education, career advancement, and flexible degree pathways.
Asset groups were aligned to:
Flexibility & convenience
Accredited public university credibility
Career outcomes & advancement
Affordability positioning
This structure allowed USU Online to scale volume while maintaining disciplined CPL targets.
Omnichannel Reinforcement
While Google Ads served as the primary acquisition engine, additional channels reinforced performance:
Google Ads: 220 leads generated at $23 cost per lead, more than 60% lower than the standard benchmark of $100 cost per lead.
Meta Lead Campaigns: 110+ leads generated at a $15 CPL, which is also 60% lower than the standard benchmark of $100 cost per lead.
LinkedIn: Targeted working professionals and adult learners
Site Retargeting: Re-engaged website visitors who did not convert
Programmatic Display: Expanded online brand visibility regionally
However, Google Search and Performance Max remained the dominant high-intent drivers.
CRM Alignment & Attribution
During campaign execution, Propellant Media collaborated with USU’s admissions and CRM teams as Salesforce was being implemented to:
Map UTM parameters to CRM lead fields
Ensure accurate source attribution
Enable click-to-enrollment visibility
Structure dashboards for program-level ROI measurement
This integration positioned USU Online for full-funnel transparency from initial click through enrollment.
Utah State University Online’s partnership with Propellant Media demonstrates the effectiveness of a structured, search-first digital acquisition model. By combining disciplined Google Search architecture, Performance Max expansion, and CRM-integrated reporting, USU Online built a scalable foundation for sustained enrollment growth across its entire online program portfolio.
University of Arizona Nursing Program
As a partner with Propellant Media since 2024, The University of Arizona College of Nursing is a nationally recognized institution known for its innovative, research-driven curriculum and commitment to excellence in healthcare education. Offering programs ranging from BSN and MSN degrees to Doctor of Nursing Practice (DNP) and PhD pathways, the school prepares students for leadership roles in nursing and healthcare.
However, the program was facing significant challenges in student recruitment due to underperforming digital campaigns managed by their previous agency. The client expressed frustration over:
Low lead volume and high cost-per-lead (CPL) from past campaigns.
Lack of transparency and communication from the prior agency.
Ineffective ad placements and audience targeting, leading to wasted ad spend.
Seeking a fresh and strategic approach, the University of Arizona Nursing Program turned to Propellant Media for help in executing a highly targeted digital campaign that would generate qualified prospective student leads at scale.
Propellant Media was given the challenge of:
Rebuilding the digital marketing strategy from the ground up, focusing on a combination of geofencing, Facebook, and Instagram advertising.
Targeting prospective nursing students across different career stages—ranging from recent high school graduates to professionals looking to advance their careers.
Optimizing ad spend to maximize lead volume while maintaining a cost-effective CPL.
Creating compelling ad creatives and messaging tailored to different audience segments.
Given the competitive nature of nursing programs nationwide, we needed to stand out in the digital space while ensuring that the campaign would deliver high-quality, engaged leads ready to explore the University of Arizona’s nursing programs.
The Tactics We Deployed
To achieve these goals, Propellant Media developed a multi-channel digital strategy, combining geofencing, social media advertising, and advanced audience segmentation.
1. Geofencing Strategy: Precision Targeting at Key Locations
We deployed advanced geofencing technology to pinpoint and engage potential students based on their real-world behaviors and locations. This included:
Geofencing Hospitals & Healthcare Facilities – Targeting working nurses and healthcare professionals who might be considering career advancement through BSN, MSN, or DNP programs.
Geofencing Community Colleges – Capturing interest from students currently pursuing associate degrees in nursing (ADN) and looking to transfer into BSN programs.
Geofencing Competitor Nursing Schools – Identifying potential students considering alternative programs and presenting University of Arizona as the best option.
2. Facebook & Instagram Advertising: Hyper-Targeted Social Campaigns
To maximize visibility and engagement, we launched a data-driven paid social strategy on Facebook and Instagram, focusing on:
Custom Audience Segments – We built precise audience segments including:
High school seniors exploring nursing as a career.
Healthcare professionals seeking advanced nursing degrees.
Working adults interested in online and flexible nursing programs.
Lookalike Audiences – We leveraged existing student data to create lookalike audiences and expand the reach to individuals with similar interests and behaviors.
Engaging Creatives & Messaging – We tested multiple ad variations highlighting:
Arizona’s world-class nursing faculty and career outcomes.
The flexibility of online and hybrid degree programs.
The growing demand for nurses nationwide, making nursing an in-demand career path.
Landing Page Optimization – We streamlined the lead capture process, ensuring that prospective students could easily submit inquiries without friction.
3. Real-Time Campaign Optimization: Maximizing Performance
Throughout the campaign, we closely monitored key performance indicators (KPIs) and made real-time optimizations to:
Reallocate budgets to the best-performing audiences.
Adjust messaging based on engagement data.
Eliminate underperforming ad placements to improve cost-efficiency.
Results From The Campaign
In just two weeks, the campaign delivered:
87 high-quality leads from prospective students.
Cost-per-lead (CPL) of just $6.00 - $9.00  – a significant improvement over previous campaigns.
Increased engagement across Facebook & Instagram, with higher click-through rates (CTR) and lower bounce rates compared to past efforts.
By combining precision geofencing with hyper-targeted social media advertising, we helped the University of Arizona College of Nursing dramatically improve lead generation efficiency while ensuring they were reaching the right audience—individuals genuinely interested in pursuing a career in nursing.
The University of Arizona Nursing Program’s partnership with Propellant Media marked a turning point in their digital marketing strategy. By leveraging geofencing and social media advertising, we helped the client:
Rebuild trust in digital marketing after frustrations with their previous agency.
Enhance student recruitment efforts through advanced targeting strategies.
Establish a scalable framework for future digital advertising campaigns.
This successful launch is just the beginning, as Propellant Media continues to refine and expand the campaign, ensuring that the University of Arizona Nursing Program continues to attract high-quality nursing candidates and strengthen its position as a leader in nursing education.
Southern California of Health Sciences
Southern California University of Health Sciences (SCU) is a private, nonprofit institution located in Whittier, California. With a long-standing reputation in integrative health, chiropractic, acupuncture, and now nursing, SCU is committed to educating the next generation of health professionals grounded in science, compassion, and whole-body wellness. As the university expanded its Bachelor of Science in Nursing (BSN) program, they needed a highly targeted digital campaign to identify and convert lifestyle-aligned prospects in a competitive Southern California market.
The Challenge
SCU’s BSN program offers a unique educational experience that blends clinical rigor with a holistic health foundation. Their challenge: reach and convert prospective students whose lifestyle, values, and academic interests aligned with this distinctive approach. Rather than broad outreach, the university needed a precision-focused campaign that would deliver quality over quantity—reaching future nurses who valued wellness, movement, balance, and purpose-driven careers.
The Strategy
Propellant Media developed a multi-channel, full-funnel campaign centered around geofencing, Meta (Facebook/Instagram) ads, and site retargeting. This approach was designed to create a 360-degree engagement loop—from physical location behavior to digital re-engagement—optimized specifically for SCU’s prospective nursing students.
Key Tactics We Executed
Geofencing Over 20 Yoga & Wellness Studios
We geofenced 20+ yoga studios and holistic wellness centers across Southern California. These locations were selected based on their relevance to SCU’s ideal student persona—individuals engaged in self-care, health, and holistic lifestyles. Mobile device data from these physical visits was used to serve follow-up digital ads, creating a localized and highly intentional awareness strategy.
Third-Party Behavioral Targeting
To complement location data, we leveraged third-party audience segments including:
Yoga & holistic health enthusiasts
Individuals exploring nursing degrees or health sciences
Users researching wellness careers or holistic living
Women aged 22–38 with patterns matching allied health program seekers
Meta (Facebook & Instagram) Geofencing + Retargeting
We ran a parallel Meta campaign targeting users who had been to geofenced locations. In addition, we launched a Meta site retargeting strategy, serving remarketing ads to users who visited SCU’s nursing program landing page but didn’t yet convert. These users saw program-specific creative, testimonials, and calls to action related to enrollment deadlines and info sessions.
Site Retargeting (Programmatic Display)
All traffic to the nursing program’s landing pages was captured and re-engaged using animated and static display ads, reminding prospective students to revisit, download brochures, or schedule a virtual consultation.
Ad Messaging Examples
“Your Path to a Holistic Nursing Career Starts Here”
“Train to Heal, Lead, and Grow—The SCU BSN Program Is Accepting Applications”
“Nursing Meets Wellness—See How SCU Is Changing the Future of Care”
ResultsThe results of the 8-week campaign showed strong top- and bottom-funnel performance across channels:

Through a smart blend of geofencing, behavioral targeting, and retargeting across both programmatic and Meta platforms, Propellant Media helped SCU connect with high-fit nursing prospects in a deeply relevant and cost-effective way. The combination of physical location data and digital behavior created a full-funnel campaign that didn’t just drive leads—it attracted students who were culturally and academically aligned with SCU’s mission. This campaign now serves as a model for future holistic health program promotions across the university’s portfolio.

Aaron King
Propellant Media (www.propellant.media) | October 2014 –Present | Founding Partner
Propellant Media is a digital marketing agency with strengths in geo-fencing technology, over-the-top & programmatic display and paid search management. There, I have developed processes for onboarding new clients and integrating data into client CRMs and dashboards including Kennesaw State University Relay GSE and Florida Gulf Coast University. I also brought multiple long-term partnership deals to the agency that has resulted in Propellant managing paid search, programmatic display, and paid social campaigns for a number of advertisers in the medical, non-profit, government, and financial services spaces.
Fusion75 LLC (www.fusion75.com) | March 2018 – Present | Founder
Fusion75 is a Marketing Technology consultancy focused on helping companies connect, aggregate, process, prepare, visualize, and analyze their data. Our service offering includes dashboard development, data pipeline building & maintenance, reporting automation, analysis, marketing technology (MarTech) consulting, Web Analytics consulting, and Paid Media Audits & Restructures.
Response Mine Interactive (RMI) | August 2014 – April 2018 | Titles Held: VP, Analytics; VP, Paid Media
Automated internal and external reporting and transitioned to cloud-based platform (Domo) which has resulted in:
Cost savings equal to approximately 2 mid-level FTE (reporting hours: 1 FTE, analysis hours 1 FTE)
Better analysis of data due to more segmented data being pulled and compiled via cloud-based ETL (Extract-Transform-Load) processes
Reduced time to onboard a new client by 75% by creating a single-ownership group accountable for the following:
Tag Management & QA via Google Tag Manager and Tealium IQ
SEM Account Onboarding via Kenshoo & Display Trafficking via Doubleclick Manager
Google Analytics implementation for new websites
Launched Display Media Trading Desk as internal capability not previously handled by RMI which created a new revenue stream for the company
Standardized Paid Media audit and offering for business development purposes resulting in approx. $600K in annualized new business revenue
Hired and developed paid media team which doubled in size
Look-Listen | May 2013 – August 2014 | Titles Held: Director, Digital Media
Achieved record online patient acquisition numbers while reducing the cost per patient for a large dental services organization
Reduced the cost of tracking by 75% (roughly $9K annualized) through vendor negotiation
Automated reporting processes and reduced timing from 8 hours to 1 hour.

PROPELLANT MEDIA
Managing Partner:
A digital marketing agency providing online advertising solutions by implementing integrated strategies including website and advertising solutions, content creation and digital marketing channels.
By optimizing a client’s paid search advertising campaign, the firm is collecting 30% more traffic based on utilizing the same budget prior to our engagement.
Helped grow Propellant Media to an 8-figure marketing agency with offices in Atlanta, GA and Charlotte NC
Lead strategy for universities including Florida Gulf Coast, Stevens Institute of Technology, and Utah State University.

Propellant Media, LLC, doing business as Propellant Media, is an independent media agency and has no parent company.
Primary office: 800 Battery Ave SE, Suite 300, Atlanta, GA 30339
Secondary office: Charlotte, NC
General phone: (877) 776-7358
Proposal contact email: justin@propellant.media
Website: propellant.media
EIN:47-4890760
2. Has your firm ever operated under a different name? Please list any previous names within the last ten (10) years.
No. Propellant Media, LLC has not operated under any other name within the last ten (10) years.
3. Give a brief description of the firm, including size and number of years in business.
Founded in 2015, Propellant Media is a performance-focused digital media agency specializing in paid media strategy, campaign execution, audience targeting, analytics, and lead generation. The firm currently employs 25 full-time staff and supports organizations across higher education, healthcare, franchise, B2B, and multi-location service sectors. Our work centers on using paid media to generate measurable outcomes through data-informed strategy, platform-specific execution, transparent reporting, and continuous optimization.

Section V.F — Overview
1. Include a Letter of Interest & Executive Summary. The letter should include a response to how you meet the qualifications listed in the scope of work. The executive summary (i.e., a proposal overview) should help the evaluation committee determine the proposal’s distinguishing characteristics.
Propellant Media appreciates the opportunity to partner with Colorado School of Mines on digital media buying and management services. We bring multi-platform paid media expertise, higher education recruitment experience, transparent billing, and CRM-informed optimization focused on improving lead volume, quality, and ROI. Our team manages campaigns across Google, LinkedIn, Meta, YouTube, Reddit, and programmatic channels, operating as an extension of internal teams while ensuring full institutional ownership of all accounts and data. We differentiate through a balance of strategic planning and hands-on execution—delivering targeted platform strategies, disciplined testing, and data-driven optimizations to support graduate enrollment growth.
Section V.G — Experience
1. Describe the firm’s relevant experience managing multi-platform digital advertising programs. If applicable, include specific examples of work in higher education or comparable enrollment-driven environments.
Propellant Media specializes in multi-platform paid digital advertising for organizations where qualified lead generation and measurable conversion outcomes are the standard for success. Our capabilities span Google Ads, LinkedIn, Meta, YouTube, Reddit, geofencing, and programmatic display and video — and we manage these channels in integrated programs, not in silos.
In higher education, we have supported graduate recruitment, adult learner acquisition, new program launches, and brand-to-demand campaign structures across institutions with complex internal stakeholder environments, CRM dependencies, and multi-cycle enrollment timelines. These engagements required the same discipline Mines demands: balancing lead volume against lead quality, managing budget pacing across simultaneous campaigns, and producing reporting that connects media activity to enrollment-relevant outcomes.
Our higher education experience maps directly to Mines' stated priorities:
Graduate lead generation across Google Search, LinkedIn, and emerging channels including Reddit and programmatic
Account architecture built around institution-owned assets, giving clients full data portability and transparency
Dashboard reporting designed for both internal marketing teams and senior leadership audiences
Creative and web collaboration with in-house teams, maintaining brand governance while improving conversion performance
Enrollment-cycle-aligned strategy, with campaign flighting and budget reallocation informed by application and inquiry data
What differentiates our approach is the combination of performance media rigor and enrollment-context fluency. We understand that a graduate prospect at the awareness stage and one 14 months into a consideration cycle require entirely different media and messaging — and we build programs that account for that.
Section V.H — Organization & Personnel
1. Identify the proposed account team, including roles, years of experience, and experience supporting institutions of similar size or complexity.
Proposed team:
Alyssa Dixon — Account Manager / Primary Liaison - Leads day-to-day communication, meeting management, project coordination, and campaign oversight. Experienced in client-facing account leadership and multi-channel campaign execution.
Brent LeVasseur — COO / Strategic Oversight - Provides strategic guidance on budget allocation, channel mix, audience segmentation, and campaign planning.
Juan Gomez — Google & Meta Specialist - Responsible for execution, optimization, testing, and performance management across search, display, YouTube, and Meta campaigns.
Aaron King — Analytics, Reporting & CRM Integration - Oversees tracking, dashboard reporting, attribution structure, and CRM-connected performance analysis.
Dylan Meetze — Programmatic Display & OTT/CTV Specialist - Supports broader media strategy and advanced audience targeting when applicable.
i. Confirm expected percentage of time allocated to this engagement.
Estimated ongoing allocation to the Mines engagement is as follows:
Alyssa Dixon — 40%
Brent LeVasseur — 25%
Juan Gomez — 35%
Aaron King — 30%
Dylan Meetze — 30%
Time allocations may shift modestly during onboarding, major launches, or enrollment pushes, but Propellant Media will maintain sufficient staffing to support campaign execution, reporting, and strategic responsiveness throughout the contract term.
ii. Describe your staff continuity plan and how you manage account transitions in the event of personnel changes.
Propellant Media maintains account continuity through shared documentation, cross-functional account access, and advance transition planning. Team members assigned to the Mines account are expected to remain on the business for a sustained period whenever possible. If a personnel change becomes necessary, Propellant Media will provide:
A structured transition plan
Shared access to all campaign documentation and optimization history
Knowledge transfer between outgoing and incoming staff
An overlap period, when possible, of at least 30 days
Advance communication and introduction of replacement personnel to Mines
Because all campaign assets, reports, dashboards, change logs, and platform configurations are managed within shared systems rather than individual employee-controlled files, continuity can be maintained with minimal disruption.
Section V.I — Digital Advertising Strategy and Campaign Management
1. Describe your strategic approach to paid digital advertising specifically for graduate-level recruitment. How do you determine platform mix, audience segmentation, and budget allocation to maximize lead generation and return on investment “ROI”?
Our strategy for graduate-level recruitment starts with program priorities, enrollment goals, audience intent, and the institution’s ability to convert inquiries into applicants and enrolled students. We develop platform mix and budget allocation based on degree type, market competitiveness, program awareness, search demand, audience specificity, and historical conversion performance.
In general:
Google Search captures high-intent demand and is prioritized where prospective students are actively searching for programs.
LinkedIn is used for professional and career-oriented graduate audiences, especially where job title, industry, or seniority targeting is valuable.
Meta and YouTube support audience development, retargeting, and demand creation through visual storytelling and mid-funnel engagement.
Reddit and other niche platforms can be effective when reaching high-intent communities or technical audiences.
Audience segmentation is built around program interest, geography, academic background, professional profile, prior website engagement, and CRM-informed audience signals where available. Budget allocation is managed dynamically based on cost efficiency, lead quality, stage in the recruitment cycle, and relative contribution to downstream outcomes. We continuously reallocate spend toward the channels, campaigns, and audiences generating the strongest performance against agreed KPIs.
2. How does your team stay current on platform updates, privacy regulations, AI-driven optimization tools, and emerging trends in digital advertising?
Propellant Media stays current through direct platform partnerships, active certifications, trade publications, product changelogs, privacy monitoring, and continuous campaign testing. We maintain current platform knowledge through:
Google Premier Partner access and agency support
Ongoing certifications across Google Ads, Meta Blueprint, and LinkedIn Marketing Solutions
Regular review of platform documentation and industry publications
Monitoring of privacy developments such as iOS updates, consent management requirements, and cookie deprecation changes
Active use and evaluation of AI-enabled tools such as smart bidding, predictive audiences, automation layers, and platform-native campaign optimization features
We do not treat platform change as a periodic review item; it is incorporated into weekly optimization and strategic planning workflows.
3. Outline your approach to ongoing campaign optimization and optimization cadence (daily/weekly). Explain how you prioritize optimizations and testing across multiple campaigns and platforms.
Propellant Media uses a structured optimization cadence that combines daily monitoring with weekly decision-making and deeper monthly performance analysis.
Daily: pacing, spend anomalies, delivery issues, conversion tracking health, audience saturation, bid volatility
Weekly: bid and budget adjustments, audience refinement, creative performance review, search query analysis, placement review, and prioritization of active tests
Monthly: broader strategic analysis, cross-channel comparisons, trend review, benchmark assessment, and recommendation planning
We prioritize optimizations based on business impact, not just metric movement. Highest priority goes to campaigns with the greatest spend, strongest enrollment relevance, or the clearest opportunity to improve lead quality or efficiency. Testing is structured so that each active campaign has a defined optimization path, with changes documented and tied to measurable hypotheses.
4. Provide a concrete example where campaign performance data led you to implement a strategic change that resulted in measurable improvement.
Propellant Media supported Kennesaw State University’s MS in Electrical and Computer Engineering program with a highly constrained $1,600/month budget. Early campaign data showed that broader targeting was inefficient, so we shifted strategy to focus exclusively on high-intent Google Search and tightly segmented LinkedIn audiences.
Through continuous optimization—negative keyword refinement, conversion-based bidding, and weekly budget reallocation—we achieved an average $30 cost per lead, significantly outperforming industry benchmarks, while maintaining consistent inquiry volume. This engagement demonstrates our ability to translate performance data into actionable optimizations that improve efficiency and outcomes in graduate recruitment campaigns.
5. Describe how clients access campaign performance data, the frequency of reporting, and your reporting formats. Please include a sample dashboard or reporting outline that communicates performance, insights, what changed/why, and what’s next.
Clients receive performance visibility through three channels:
24/7 live dashboard access
Monthly performance reporting
Direct native platform access
Our reporting is designed to show not only performance, but decision-making. Standard monthly reporting includes:
Spend by platform and campaign
Leads / conversions and cost efficiency
Trends versus prior period
Quality observations where CRM data is available
What changed during the period
Why those changes were made
What was learned
What is recommended next
A sample dashboard or reporting outline is included in the appendix.
6. Confirm the institution retains administrator ownership and access to all advertising and measurement accounts used for this engagement, during and after the engagement.
Confirmed. Colorado School of Mines will retain administrator ownership and access to all advertising and measurement accounts used for this engagement during the full term of the contract and after contract conclusion. Propellant Media will operate through delegated access structures and will not require ownership transfer of institutional accounts, data, audiences, pixels, or tracking configurations.
7. Do you have experience integrating digital advertising performance tracking with Slate or similar CRM/enrollment systems? Please describe.
Yes. Propellant Media has experience aligning paid media reporting with Slate and similar CRM/enrollment systems in order to improve attribution, audience strategy, and downstream performance analysis. Our work has included form-source tracking, CRM-informed audience creation, and matching media activity with enrollment funnel outcomes such as inquiry, application, and admitted student progression.
8. Include if you have any options for connecting your ad platform activity to the institution’s CRM (e.g., Slate).
Yes. Depending on the institution’s technical environment and permissions, we can support CRM-connected measurement through:
Confirmation-page conversion tracking
CRM field mapping and source tagging
Secure audience uploads for lookalike or remarketing strategies
Periodic data matching between CRM outcomes and ad platform activity
Dashboard layers that compare media performance with downstream enrollment funnel indicators
Where direct API access is limited, we can still support structured imports/exports and reporting workflows that connect ad spend to CRM outcomes.
Section V.J — Platform Access and Onboarding
1. Describe your process for setting up user accounts and permissions, and how you ensure secure access for multiple client users.
All accounts remain owned by Mines. Propellant Media is added through platform-native agency access structures, such as Google Ads MCC, LinkedIn Campaign Manager permissions, and Meta Business Manager partner access. Access is assigned by role and limited to the minimum level required. We recommend structured permissions for administrators, day-to-day marketing stakeholders, and reporting-only users. We also document account access during onboarding and review permissions periodically to ensure secure access and continuity.
2. Does your solution integrate with Slate or other enrollment/CRM systems? If so, describe the integration process and any limitations.
Yes. Propellant Media can work with Slate and similar CRM systems through conversion tracking, source tagging, audience syncing, and periodic data matching, depending on the institution’s configuration. Integration typically includes discovery, requirements review, implementation planning, testing, and validation. Limitations generally relate to API access, internal IT availability, naming conventions, and available CRM fields. We identify those dependencies early and build the reporting approach accordingly.
3. How do clients access performance data and administrative reports?
Clients access performance data through:
24/7 live dashboard access
Monthly reporting summaries
Direct native platform access to owned ad accounts
Additional ad hoc reporting upon request
Administrative reports, billing reconciliation, and change documentation can be delivered through shared folders, dashboard notes, or standardized monthly reporting packages.
Section V.K — Customer Service
1. Describe your approach to onboarding a new client, specifically a higher education institution like Mines.
Our onboarding process is designed to create continuity quickly while giving the institution full visibility into strategy, accounts, reporting, and payment workflows. For higher education clients, onboarding typically includes kickoff planning, historical campaign review, platform access confirmation, tracking validation, audience and conversion review, reporting/dashboard setup, billing workflow setup, and initial optimization planning aligned to recruitment priorities.
2. How do you coordinate access to client ad accounts and payment processes during onboarding?
We begin by confirming account ownership, administrative contacts, existing permissions, billing structures, and any platform-specific dependencies. We then document required access levels, validate tracking, and align invoice/payment workflows so that media spend and agency fees remain clearly separated and reconcilable.
3. How do you handle potential delays or issues during the onboarding process?
We identify dependencies early, assign owners to each onboarding step, and maintain a shared onboarding checklist. If delays arise, we escalate them quickly, document the issue, propose a workaround where possible, and communicate timing impacts clearly to the client team.
4. Describe your approach to ongoing support after onboarding is complete.
After onboarding, support shifts into a structured operating rhythm of campaign monitoring, scheduled reporting, optimization reviews, strategic recommendations, and responsive day-to-day communication. Our goal is to function as an extension of the client marketing team rather than a separate vendor silo.
5. How do you manage campaign monitoring, optimization, and reporting post-launch?
Campaigns are actively monitored for pacing, delivery, tracking health, and performance. Optimization occurs on a recurring cadence, with decisions documented and reflected in monthly reporting and review meetings.
6. How do you proactively communicate updates, recommendations, or emerging issues to your client team?
We provide proactive communication through regular meetings, email recaps, dashboard notes, and direct outreach when issues or opportunities arise. Significant changes, risks, or performance shifts are communicated promptly, along with recommended action.
7. How do you conduct performance reviews and planning sessions with clients?
We conduct monthly performance reviews that cover results, insights, tests, changes made, program-level patterns, risks, and next-step recommendations. Broader planning sessions can also be scheduled around enrollment cycles, budget shifts, or new program priorities.
8. How do you track customer satisfaction?
We track satisfaction through account feedback, retention, responsiveness monitoring, recurring client check-ins, and periodic review of service quality, reporting usefulness, and strategic alignment.
9. What is your average response time for support?
Our standard response time for routine support requests is within one business day, with urgent matters addressed more quickly.
10. What is the average time to resolution?
Resolution time varies by issue type, but most standard platform, reporting, and campaign issues are addressed within one to three business days. Time-sensitive matters are prioritized immediately.
11. How can we escalate issues or urgent needs?
Urgent issues can be escalated directly to the Account Manager and, if needed, to executive leadership. Escalation contacts will be provided at kickoff and included in onboarding documentation.
12. How can we request support for urgent needs?
Urgent support requests may be made by email, direct phone call, or through the designated account contacts. We establish preferred escalation and urgent-response procedures with each client during onboarding.
13. Describe your communication structure, including meeting cadence, escalation protocols, and how you document campaign recommendations and changes. How do you foster a collaborative partnership with internal marketing teams?
Our communication structure includes a primary account lead, backup support contacts, regular status meetings, monthly performance reviews, and documented campaign changes. Recommendations are summarized in writing and reflected in reporting, dashboards, or meeting recaps. We foster collaboration by maintaining transparency, aligning recommendations to client goals, and working within the client’s existing planning and approval processes.
Section V.L — Additional Guidance
1. Besides the services listed in the scope of work, does your firm provide any other service that would be beneficial to Mines?
The following services would be beneficial to the university:
CRO & Landing Page Consultin
Creative & Video Services
2. Besides what is listed in the Scope of Work, are there additional features or services that your firm provides that could be beneficial to the Mines Community? Please provide full details.
Propellant Media also provides complementary services that may be beneficial to Mines in future phases or separate engagements, including:
Search engine optimization
Landing page optimization and conversion rate optimization
CRM marketing support
Streaming audio and podcast media planning
Digital out-of-home planning
These services are not included in the current requested scope unless specifically approved by Mines, but they may provide future value if the University chooses to expand its recruitment or brand-awareness strategy.
3. Is there any additional information that Mines should be aware of or anything that you believe Mines has overlooked regarding this project?
One consideration that may materially affect campaign performance is the degree of connection between paid media reporting and downstream enrollment outcomes. If Mines has the ability to align campaign data with CRM milestones such as inquiry quality, application start, application completion, admission, and enrollment, the University will be in a stronger position to optimize for true recruitment outcomes rather than top-of-funnel conversions alone. Propellant Media would welcome the opportunity to support that measurement framework in collaboration with Mines’ internal stakeholders.
Section V.M — Training
1. Describe, in detail, how the ongoing administrator support/training will be handled after the onboarding phase and initial training is performed.
Post-onboarding support and training will be handled through a combination of live support, written documentation, and on-demand recorded guidance. Propellant Media will provide:
Live screen-share training sessions for dashboard use, reporting interpretation, and account review
Recorded walkthroughs for repeatable internal training
Written documentation for dashboards, naming conventions, and reporting logic
Refresher sessions when personnel change or reporting needs evolve
There is no additional charge for routine administrator support tied to the reporting and campaign management services provided under this engagement.
Section VI — Pricing
A. Commission Structure
1. Describe your commission-based pricing model for digital media services.
Propellant Media charges a 12% management fee calculated as a percentage of total media spend. This fee is drawn from within the stated budget — not added on top — ensuring Mines' total investment stays within the approved range. A separate annual technology and reporting platform fee of $9,000 covers dashboard infrastructure, reporting tools, and CRM-connected attribution reporting. All media costs are passed through to platforms at actual cost with no markup.
2. Are there any tiered or volume-based discounts applied to media spend? If so, provide details.
Propellant Media does not apply tiered or volume-based discounts to the management fee. The 12% rate is consistent across the full $500,000–$800,000 budget range. This ensures pricing predictability and eliminates incentives to inflate spend to reach a discount threshold.
3. Are there minimum media spend requirements to qualify for commission rates? If yes, provide details.
No minimum media spend requirements apply. The 12% management fee applies uniformly regardless of how Mines allocates spend across platforms or whether budget is reduced within the contract term.
4. Identify any additional fees, charges, or pass-through costs not included in the commission/management fees.
The only fee outside the 12% management fee is an annual technology and reporting platform fee of $9,000 ($750/month). This covers Propellant Media's proprietary dashboard infrastructure, custom Looker Studio reporting buildout, TapClicks integration, and CRM attribution reporting tools. There are no other fees. Account transition, audit, pixel review, and setup are fully waived. Platform media costs are passed through at actual cost with no markup.
5. Describe your process for tracking media spend in order to accurately assess commissions/management fees.
Media spend is tracked in real time through each platform's native reporting — Google Ads, LinkedIn Campaign Manager, Meta Business Manager, and Reddit Ads Manager. Monthly spend is reconciled against platform delivery reports and reflected in a single itemized invoice to Mines. The invoice separates media spend by platform, the 12% management fee calculation, and the technology fee line item. Mines has 24/7 access to the live dashboard and can verify platform spend independently at any time through its own administrator access.
6. What is your standard payment schedule for digital media services? Please specify timing and frequency of invoicing.
Propellant Media invoices monthly. Invoices are issued at the beginning of each month covering the upcoming month's estimated media spend and the applicable management fee. Final reconciliation against actual delivery is reflected in the following month's invoice. All invoicing operates on NET 30 payment terms, consistent with Mines' confirmed payment terms in Addendum Q35.
7. In cases where prepaid campaigns do not achieve agreed-upon metrics, do you provide reimbursement, credit, or adjustment toward future campaigns? Describe the process.
Propellant Media does not guarantee specific metric outcomes, as platform performance is subject to auction dynamics, creative performance, and enrollment market conditions outside our direct control. However, any unspent media dollars resulting from underdelivery, paused campaigns, or budget adjustments are fully credited back to Mines in the following month's invoice. Management fees are assessed only on actual media spend delivered — not on budgeted amounts.
8. Do your commission/management fees increase on an annual basis? If so, please specify typical percentage increases.
The 12% management fee is fixed for the initial contract term (Year 1, July 2026 – June 2027). The technology fee of $9,000/year is also fixed for Year 1.

<w:tcPr><w:tcBorders><w:top w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:left w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:bottom w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/><w:right w:color="000000" w:space="0" w:sz="12" w:val="single"/></w:tcBorders><w:tcMar><w:top w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:left w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:bottom w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/><w:right w:w="0.0" w:type="dxa"/></w:tcMar><w:vAlign w:val="top"/></w:tcPr><w:p w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidP="00000000" w:rsidRDefault="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000" w14:paraId="0000069A"><w:pPr><w:spacing w:after="240" w:before="240" w:lineRule="auto"/><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/></w:rPr></w:pPr><w:r w:rsidDel="00000000" w:rsidR="00000000" w:rsidRPr="00000000"><w:rPr><w:rFonts w:ascii="Poppins" w:cs="Poppins" w:eastAsia="Poppins" w:hAnsi="Poppins"/><w:sz w:val="20"/><w:szCs w:val="20"/><w:rtl w:val="0"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">Mutually agreed upon at renewal; any adjustment reflects scope changes, not standard escalators
9. Are there circumstances where fees could increase outside of the standard annual adjustment, such as changes in performance metrics or other measurable outcomes? Please describe.
No. Propellant Media's management fee does not increase based on performance metrics, campaign volume, platform count, or any other variable outcome. The 12% fee is fixed for the contract term. Fee adjustments in subsequent years, if any, are negotiated at renewal and reflect material changes in scope — not performance-based escalators.
10. Describe whether the agency can serve as a billing pass-through for platform spend (single-invoice model) versus institution-paid platform billing.
Yes. Propellant Media can serve as the pass-through billing entity, paying Google, LinkedIn, Meta, and Reddit directly on Mines' behalf and issuing a single monthly invoice that itemizes media spend by platform and management fee separately. This is Mines' stated preference per Addendum Q15, Q91, and Q111, and it is Propellant Media's standard operating model. Alternatively, if Mines prefers to pay platforms directly, Propellant Media can operate within that structure and invoice only for management and technology fees. Either model is supported.
11. Detail your pricing model and media buying structure. Clearly describe how management fees are calculated and how media spend is reconciled. Please provide a comprehensive cost proposal including all fees.
Propellant Media's pricing model is straightforward and fully transparent:
Management Fee: 12% of total media spend, drawn from within the approved annual budget
Technology & Reporting Fee: $9,000/year ($750/month), covering dashboard infrastructure, reporting buildout, and CRM attribution tools
Media Spend: Passed through to platforms at actual cost — no markup, no hidden charges
Setup/Transition: Fully waived

Reconciliation process: At month close, Propellant Media pulls delivery data from each platform's native reporting, compiles actual spend by platform and campaign, calculates the 12% management fee on actual spend delivered, and issues a single itemized invoice to Mines within 5 business days. The invoice distinguishes media spend by platform, management fee, and technology fee as separate line items. Mines can independently verify all figures through its own administrator access to each ad account.
B. Incentives and Discounts
1. Do you offer any performance-based incentives, rebates, or discounts for exceeding campaign KPIs or volume thresholds? If yes, provide details.
Propellant Media does not offer performance-based rebates or discounts. Our pricing model is designed to eliminate conflicts of interest — the 12% fee applies uniformly regardless of whether performance exceeds or falls short of benchmarks. This ensures our team is always optimizing toward Mines' enrollment outcomes rather than toward fee-triggering thresholds. The incentive is built into the structure: strong performance leads to contract renewal.
2. Are there opportunities for cost savings or efficiencies that Mines could realize through your pricing model?
Yes. Several efficiencies are built into this model:
No setup costs — the waived transition and audit fee means 100% of Mines' Year 1 budget goes to working media and management from day one
No platform markups — every media dollar is deployed at actual platform cost
Fee scales with media, not scope — if Mines reduces budget mid-year, the management fee decreases proportionally
Technology fee is fixed — as media spend grows or campaign complexity increases, the $9,000 technology fee does not scale, making it progressively more cost-efficient over a multi-year partnership
C. Transparency and Reporting
1. Describe how commission/management fees are documented and reported to the client.
Management fees are documented on every monthly invoice as a separate line item, showing the total media spend delivered, the 12% calculation, and the resulting fee amount. The fee breakdown is also visible in the live reporting dashboard, which shows media spend and management fee as distinct figures. No fee is ever bundled into or obscured within media spend line items.
2. Will Mines have visibility into media spend versus fees in real time or via reporting dashboards?
Yes. Mines' live Looker Studio dashboard displays media spend by platform and campaign in real time, updated as platforms report delivery. Management fees and the technology fee are reflected separately. Mines also has direct administrator access to each ad platform account and can pull native spend reports independently at any time without Propellant Media involvement.
3. Clearly delineate how commission/management fees are separated from actual platform media spend in billing and reporting.
Every invoice and every dashboard view maintains a clear separation between three figures: actual platform media spend, 12% management fee, and $750 monthly technology fee. These are never combined into a single line item.
a. Confirm that invoices and performance reports distinguish between agency fees and the net media investment allocated to each platform and campaign.
Confirmed. Every monthly invoice issued to Mines will itemize net media investment by platform (Google, LinkedIn, Meta, Reddit, and any additional channels) and by campaign where relevant, with the management fee and technology fee shown as separate line items. Performance reports mirror this structure — media spend and agency fees are always distinct, never aggregated.
